        Idea:
# Robust Review Phase for Megaplan (Heavy Mode Only)

## Context

Megaplan is a planning/execution harness with phases: prep → plan → critique → gate → (revise → critique → gate)* → finalize → execute → review. Critique already has a "robust" mode where it runs multiple focused checks in parallel (`megaplan/parallel_critique.py` + `megaplan/checks.py`), each as an independent sub-agent with its own session. We validated on ~15 random SWE-bench failures that the current single-call review phase misses ~65% of salvageable failures because it anchors to the plan's success criteria instead of the original issue text, and because it evaluates everything in one call without forced enumeration.

## Goal

Add a **robust review** mode that mirrors the robust critique architecture: multiple focused, parallel, issue-anchored checks that produce structured flags with rework directives, which feed back through the existing gate → revise/re-execute loop.

**Standard robustness mode must remain exactly as today.** This design is heavy-mode only.

## What exists today (don't break)

- `megaplan/prompts/review.py` — single-call review prompt; validates execution against finalize.json criteria/task_verdicts/sense_check_verdicts. Works well as the baseline. Keep it.
- `megaplan/prompts/critique.py` + `megaplan/parallel_critique.py` + `megaplan/checks.py` — the pattern we're mirroring. Read these to understand the architecture.
- `megaplan/handlers.py` `handle_review()` — entry point. Currently calls a single worker.
- The gate → revise/re-execute loop already exists with max iteration cap and ESCALATE semantics.
- `intent_and_notes_block(state)` contains the original issue text — crucial input for issue-anchored checks.

## What needs to exist

### 1. New check table for review

New file `megaplan/review_checks.py` mirroring `megaplan/checks.py`. Define `REVIEW_CHECKS` with these entries (core + extended tiers). Each check has `id`, `question`, `guidance`, `category`, `default_severity`, `tier`:

**Core tier (always run in heavy mode):**
- `coverage` — Enumerate every concrete failing example, symptom, or "X should Y" statement in the issue. For each, point to the specific line in the diff that addresses it. Flag any not covered. Subsumes intent/opt-in/default and accepted-changes concerns.
- `placement` — Trace the call path from the failing input to where we made the change. Is the fix at the source of the bug or a downstream consumer? Would a maintainer put it here or elsewhere?
- `parity` — Find 2–3 structurally similar classes/functions in the same module. Enumerate the methods/overrides/conventions they share. Does our new code follow the same patterns? If not, why not? (Highest new-leverage check.)
- `simplicity` — What's the smallest possible change that would make the reported issue's example work? Compare against our diff. Every extra line must justify its existence against a specific requirement in the issue.

**Extended tier (heavy mode with extended robustness):**
- `dead_guard` — For every new `if x:` / `try:` block added by the patch, trace whether the guarded branch is actually reachable given how callers invoke this function. Flag guards that never fire.
- `adjacent_calls` — What other code calls the changed function or uses the same pattern? Would the same bug affect them?
- `parser_source` — If the bug is in parsing/transformation, is the fix at the source of the bug or at a downstream consumer?
- `return_value_downstream` — For any value the patch returns or constructs in an error path, trace where it flows next. Will the downstream consumer handle it correctly?

Also include a `checks_for_robustness()` function mirroring `megaplan/checks.py`.

### 2. Mechanical pre-checks (no LLM, run before parallel review)

Add a new module `megaplan/review_mechanical.py` or inline in handlers:
- `source_touch` — Does the diff touch at least one non-test file in the package? Reject test-only diffs.
- `diff_size_sanity` — Compare diff size (lines/hunks/files) to a rough expectation from the issue (number of code snippets × ~10 lines). Flag >3x or <0.3x.
- `dead_guard_static` — For new `if x:` blocks, AST-parse the changed file, find the enclosing function, check whether `x` has a callable signature at the call site where it could be None/False. This is a heuristic; don't overreach.

These are **advisory flags**, not blocks. They populate a `pre_check_flags` array that the LLM review checks see as input.

### 3. Parallel review runner

New file `megaplan/parallel_review.py`, mirroring `megaplan/parallel_critique.py`. Same architecture: `ThreadPoolExecutor`, one agent per check, each with its own session via `SessionDB`, writes findings to a pre-populated template slot. Concurrency of 2 (same as critique). Include the MiniMax→OpenRouter fallback pattern.

Important: each check agent gets **the original issue text + the full git diff + the finalize.json artifacts**, nothing else. No plan, no critique history, no gate summary. The whole point is that these checks are anchored to the issue, not the plan.

### 4. Prompt builders for single-check review

In `megaplan/prompts/review.py`, add:
- `single_check_review_prompt(state, plan_dir, root, check, output_path)` — mirrors `single_check_critique_prompt` — single focused prompt for one check.
- `_write_single_check_review_template(plan_dir, state, check, filename)` — pre-populates the output file with the check's ID.
- `_build_review_checks_template(plan_dir, state, checks)` — builds the per-check template list.

Keep `_review_prompt()` as-is. It still runs as the "criteria_verdict" check in heavy mode.

### 5. Handler integration

In `megaplan/handlers.py` `handle_review()`:
- Read `configured_robustness(state)`
- If `standard` or `light` → run the existing single-call review unchanged
- If `heavy` → run the mechanical pre-checks, then run `parallel_review()` across REVIEW_CHECKS + the existing criteria-based review-as-check. Merge all flags into one `review.json`

The output shape adds a `checks` array alongside the existing `review_verdict`, `criteria`, `issues`, `rework_items`, `task_verdicts`, `sense_check_verdicts`, `summary`. Back-compat: existing consumers that only look at `review_verdict` / `rework_items` keep working.

### 6. Feedback loop — how flags flow back

This is the tricky part. The review runs after execute. If it raises blocking flags, where does control go?

**Decision: reuse the existing gate → revise/re-execute loop, not a new review-specific loop.**

Concretely:
- If `review_verdict == "needs_rework"` with blocking flags, the handler writes them to `rework_items` as today.
- The execute loop in `evals/run_evals.py` already handles `needs_rework` by going back to revise or re-execute based on the rework type. No new routing logic.
- Add a new field `rework_directive.source = "review_{check_id}"` so the executor knows the provenance and can prioritize.
- **Max review iterations cap at 2** (configurable). After 2 rounds of needs_rework from the review, escalate. Otherwise the same check can fire forever on tasks the model can't solve.
- Review flags **cannot re-raise concerns the gate already settled**. Before raising a coverage/placement/parity flag, the review check must check `gate.settled_decisions` — if the concern matches a settled decision, downgrade to "significant" (info) rather than "blocking". This prevents the gate-override deadlock we hit before.

### 7. Severity ladder (mirrors critique)

Each review flag has:
- `blocking` — triggers `needs_rework`, forces another revise/execute pass
- `significant` — logged, shown to gate for future iterations, doesn't auto-block
- `minor` — info only, aggregated into summary

### 8. Flag tracking across review iterations

Mirror critique's flag registry. Each review check's findings get IDs like `REVIEW-COV-001`. Status transitions open → verified → resolved across iterations, just like critique flags. Read `megaplan/_core` flag registry helpers — reuse them, don't duplicate.

### 9. Schema updates

Add a review checks schema fragment alongside the existing review schema in `schemas/` (wherever step schemas live — look at `STEP_SCHEMA_FILENAMES` in `megaplan/workers.py`). The schema should allow the existing top-level fields PLUS a new `checks` array with per-check findings.

### 10. Tests

- Unit tests for `REVIEW_CHECKS` structure and `checks_for_robustness()` behavior
- Unit test that standard robustness still calls the single-call review (regression guard)
- Unit test for mechanical pre-checks (source_touch, diff_size_sanity)
- Integration test: feed a known-bad patch through heavy review, assert it raises the expected flag
- Integration test: feed a known-good patch through heavy review, assert no blocking flags

Use the existing test infrastructure patterns.

## Generalization beyond review

While implementing, keep these generalizations in mind (don't implement them now, but design so they're easy later):

1. **`checks_for_robustness()` pattern** should be extractable to a shared utility — same function body in checks.py and review_checks.py should be one function.
2. **Parallel focused-agent runner** (`parallel_critique.py` + `parallel_review.py`) should be refactorable to a single `parallel_phase_runner.py` that both delegate to. Not in this PR, but leave clean seams.
3. **Template-file-with-preloaded-IDs pattern** should be documented as "the preferred way to get structured output" in a short comment at the top of `parallel_review.py`.
4. **Mechanical pre-checks should be runnable standalone** so they can be reused by critique, finalize, or any other phase that wants cheap advisory checks.

## Out of scope for this plan

- Automatic golden-patch comparison for false-negative detection (we already have `auto_improve/check_false_negatives.py`)
- Running these checks on tasks that already passed
- A/B evaluation of which checks actually earn their spot (that's a separate measurement exercise)
- Any changes to critique, plan, execute, prep, or finalize phases
- Any changes to standard or light robustness mode
- New top-level phases between review and execute

## Success criteria

- Heavy mode review produces a `checks` array with one entry per REVIEW_CHECK
- Standard mode review produces byte-identical output to today for the same input
- Blocking flags flow through the existing rework loop without new routing logic
- Flag iteration cap prevents infinite review→re-execute loops
- Unit + integration tests pass
- Code follows the same patterns as `parallel_critique.py` and `checks.py` — a reader familiar with critique should find nothing surprising in the review code

- MODIFIED: `megaplan/prompts/review.py` (add single-check helpers, don't touch existing `_review_prompt`)
- MODIFIED: `megaplan/handlers.py` `handle_review()` (branch on robustness)
- MODIFIED: `schemas/review.json` (or equivalent — add checks array)
- NEW: tests under `tests/` matching existing patterns

  "meta_commentary": "Heavy-mode robust review mirrors robust critique. Three critical invariants dominate execution: (1) standard mode must be byte-identical \\u2014 the regression test compares bytes, not keys; any stray whitespace in _review_prompt or the standard handler branch fails it. (2) collect_git_diff_patch MUST include untracked files (FLAG-007 lock-down) \\u2014 use `git add -N .` + `git diff HEAD` + `git reset` because `validate_execution_evidence` at megaplan/evaluation.py:109-175 treats untracked files as real work. (3) heavy_criteria_review_prompt is built from scratch (not a _review_prompt wrapper) and must exclude plan/plan-metadata/gate-summary/execution-summary/execution-audit (FLAG-005/006 lock-down). The grep-assertion test in T11(f) is the load-bearing regression guard \\u2014 do not weaken it. The plan deliberately diverges from the brief's literal 'keep _review_prompt as criteria_verdict' wording (GATE-SD-009) because the brief is internally contradictory: '_review_prompt as-is' and 'issue-anchored context only' cannot both hold. Document this in the new prompt's docstring. Execution order can parallelize: T2/T3/T4 are independent after T1; T5/T6 parallel after T1/T3; T7 depends on T3+T5; T8 on T4+T5+T7; T9 on T5; T10 on T2+T6+T8+T9; T11/T12 last. Thread the new `robustness` parameter through BOTH handle_review callsites \\u2014 easy to miss one. parallel_review must use _toolsets_for_phase('review') (file-only), NOT critique's file+web \\u2014 review is read-only. REVIEW_CHECKS stays at exactly 8 entries; criteria-verdict is scheduled as an extra future inside parallel_review via a `_run_criteria_verdict` helper, not as a registry entry. Flag ids use REVIEW-<CHECK_ID>-NNN. Share _synthesize_flags_from_checks between critique and review \\u2014 don't copy-paste. The sentinel task_id='REVIEW' in heavy-mode rework items must be tolerated by _merge_review_verdicts (which only strict-checks task_verdicts). No changes to evals/run_evals.py routing \\u2014 the existing gate\\u2192revise/execute loop handles needs_rework unchanged.",

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the mobile action-surface plan is technically incorrect for the current component structure. `submissioncarouselcard` hides `footercontent` below the `md` breakpoint (`src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx:219`), while `submissionscarouselpage` also hides the score panel on mobile via `asidecontent={<div classname="hidden md:block">...` and passes `hideactions` to `scorepanel` at `src/pages/submissionscarouselpage.tsx:250-263,347`. reusing that pattern for entrypage would leave mobile users with no navigation controls and no scoring controls. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: i checked the sign-in callback path again. `submissionslayout` already wires `usescoring(..., { onrequiresignin: () => pagemachine.dispatch({ type: 'open_sign_in_modal' }) })` at `src/pages/submissionslayout.tsx:38-40`, and `scorepanel` still has its own `onrequiresignin` prop. the revised step 4.2 is acceptable only if it explicitly rewires that prop to `pagemachine.dispatch`; otherwise removing local modal state would orphan the direct ui callback. the plan hints at this, but it remains easy to misread because the scoring-context callback and the scorepanel prop are separate mechanisms. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the new diff-helper proposal against the repo's existing execution-evidence semantics. `validate_execution_evidence()` in `megaplan/evaluation.py:109-175` uses `git status --short`, so untracked files count as real changed files today, but step 3 proposes `collect_git_diff_patch(project_dir)` via `git diff head`; by git behavior that will not include untracked files, so heavy review could miss exactly the newly created files that execute and review already treat as part of the patch. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-success-criteria-well-prioritized-and-verifiable: are the success criteria well-prioritized and verifiable?: mobile behavior is still under-prioritized relative to the actual risk in this repo. the user constraint specifically mentions the mobile bottom nav, but the only mobile-specific success criterion left is 'mobile scoring ux is coherent' at `info` level. given that the plan currently removes the working mobile nav, there should be at least a `should` or `must` criterion stating that mobile users retain navigation and scoring controls. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] criteria-prompt: criteria prompt: v3 replaces `_review_prompt()` in heavy mode with a new `heavy_criteria_review_prompt`, so the plan no longer follows the brief's explicit requirement that `_review_prompt()` remain the heavy-mode `criteria_verdict` check. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements: did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: a new critical issue is introduced on mobile. step 4.9 says to remove entrypage's mobile sticky bottom nav and 'use the same `footercontent` slot in submissioncarouselcard', but `src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx:217-221` renders `footercontent` inside `classname="hidden md:block"`. in the current repo, the mobile sticky nav at `src/pages/entrypage.tsx:283-340` is the only mobile surface for back/next and the score toggle, so the revised plan would remove working mobile controls and replace them with a slot that is explicitly hidden on phones. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements: did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the plan still contradicts itself on mobile behavior. assumption #3 says the browse-mode mobile score toggle will be preserved, but step 4.9 removes the entire mobile sticky nav that contains that toggle in `src/pages/entrypage.tsx:314-330`. the implementer cannot satisfy both instructions as written. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements: did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: checked step 7 against the brief's explicit prompt-builder requirement. the revision fixes the old context leak by introducing a brand-new `heavy_criteria_review_prompt`, but the brief said `_review_prompt()` should stay as-is and still run as the `criteria_verdict` check in heavy mode; v3 solves the problem by replacing that heavy-mode criteria path instead of reusing the baseline review prompt, which is still a material divergence from the requested design. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] diff-capture: diff capture: the proposed `collect_git_diff_patch(project_dir)` helper uses `git diff head`, which will miss untracked files even though the existing execution-evidence path already treats untracked files as real changed work. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure: does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: if the implementation really wants `footercontent` to carry mobile actions, the plan must also include a supporting change in `src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx` or render a separate mobile control surface outside the card. right now step 4.9 removes the mobile nav but does not list `submissioncarouselcard.tsx:217-221` as a place that must change, so the required glue for mobile controls is missing. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] execute-phase-contract: early-killed baselines pass truncated output to the execute worker, which may produce lower-quality diagnosis than full output. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them: find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: i checked the skip caller path against the real hook behavior and `removefromqueue(entryid)` looks redundant. `scoring.skipentry(entryid)` optimistically adds the id to `skippedentryids` in `src/hooks/usescoring.ts:245-249`, and `usecarouselqueue` already reactively filters skipped entries from `submissions[]` in `src/hooks/usecarouselqueue.ts:154-165`. calling `removefromqueue()` immediately afterward is safe, but it duplicates an existing state transition and should be justified as an explicit ux optimization rather than required logic. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them: find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the direct ui sign-in caller is still important here. `scorepanel` invokes its own `onrequiresignin` prop when an unauthenticated user interacts with the score ui (`src/components/scorepanel.tsx:499-515`), which is independent of the scoring hook's internal `requireauth()` path. the plan should be read as requiring that caller to dispatch `open_sign_in_modal`; otherwise one caller remains broken even if `submitscore()` is correctly wired. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] handler-boilerplate: _run_worker() pseudocode hardcodes iteration=state['iteration'] but handle_plan and handle_revise use different failure iteration values. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] handler-boilerplate: same issue as correctness-1: failure iteration would be wrong for plan/revise handlers. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)

        Idea:
# Robust Review Phase for Megaplan (Heavy Mode Only)

## Context

Megaplan is a planning/execution harness with phases: prep → plan → critique → gate → (revise → critique → gate)* → finalize → execute → review. Critique already has a "robust" mode where it runs multiple focused checks in parallel (`megaplan/parallel_critique.py` + `megaplan/checks.py`), each as an independent sub-agent with its own session. We validated on ~15 random SWE-bench failures that the current single-call review phase misses ~65% of salvageable failures because it anchors to the plan's success criteria instead of the original issue text, and because it evaluates everything in one call without forced enumeration.

## Goal

Add a **robust review** mode that mirrors the robust critique architecture: multiple focused, parallel, issue-anchored checks that produce structured flags with rework directives, which feed back through the existing gate → revise/re-execute loop.

**Standard robustness mode must remain exactly as today.** This design is heavy-mode only.

## What exists today (don't break)

- `megaplan/prompts/review.py` — single-call review prompt; validates execution against finalize.json criteria/task_verdicts/sense_check_verdicts. Works well as the baseline. Keep it.
- `megaplan/prompts/critique.py` + `megaplan/parallel_critique.py` + `megaplan/checks.py` — the pattern we're mirroring. Read these to understand the architecture.
- `megaplan/handlers.py` `handle_review()` — entry point. Currently calls a single worker.
- The gate → revise/re-execute loop already exists with max iteration cap and ESCALATE semantics.
- `intent_and_notes_block(state)` contains the original issue text — crucial input for issue-anchored checks.

## What needs to exist

### 1. New check table for review

New file `megaplan/review_checks.py` mirroring `megaplan/checks.py`. Define `REVIEW_CHECKS` with these entries (core + extended tiers). Each check has `id`, `question`, `guidance`, `category`, `default_severity`, `tier`:

**Core tier (always run in heavy mode):**
- `coverage` — Enumerate every concrete failing example, symptom, or "X should Y" statement in the issue. For each, point to the specific line in the diff that addresses it. Flag any not covered. Subsumes intent/opt-in/default and accepted-changes concerns.
- `placement` — Trace the call path from the failing input to where we made the change. Is the fix at the source of the bug or a downstream consumer? Would a maintainer put it here or elsewhere?
- `parity` — Find 2–3 structurally similar classes/functions in the same module. Enumerate the methods/overrides/conventions they share. Does our new code follow the same patterns? If not, why not? (Highest new-leverage check.)
- `simplicity` — What's the smallest possible change that would make the reported issue's example work? Compare against our diff. Every extra line must justify its existence against a specific requirement in the issue.

**Extended tier (heavy mode with extended robustness):**
- `dead_guard` — For every new `if x:` / `try:` block added by the patch, trace whether the guarded branch is actually reachable given how callers invoke this function. Flag guards that never fire.
- `adjacent_calls` — What other code calls the changed function or uses the same pattern? Would the same bug affect them?
- `parser_source` — If the bug is in parsing/transformation, is the fix at the source of the bug or at a downstream consumer?
- `return_value_downstream` — For any value the patch returns or constructs in an error path, trace where it flows next. Will the downstream consumer handle it correctly?

Also include a `checks_for_robustness()` function mirroring `megaplan/checks.py`.

### 2. Mechanical pre-checks (no LLM, run before parallel review)

Add a new module `megaplan/review_mechanical.py` or inline in handlers:
- `source_touch` — Does the diff touch at least one non-test file in the package? Reject test-only diffs.
- `diff_size_sanity` — Compare diff size (lines/hunks/files) to a rough expectation from the issue (number of code snippets × ~10 lines). Flag >3x or <0.3x.
- `dead_guard_static` — For new `if x:` blocks, AST-parse the changed file, find the enclosing function, check whether `x` has a callable signature at the call site where it could be None/False. This is a heuristic; don't overreach.

These are **advisory flags**, not blocks. They populate a `pre_check_flags` array that the LLM review checks see as input.

### 3. Parallel review runner

New file `megaplan/parallel_review.py`, mirroring `megaplan/parallel_critique.py`. Same architecture: `ThreadPoolExecutor`, one agent per check, each with its own session via `SessionDB`, writes findings to a pre-populated template slot. Concurrency of 2 (same as critique). Include the MiniMax→OpenRouter fallback pattern.

Important: each check agent gets **the original issue text + the full git diff + the finalize.json artifacts**, nothing else. No plan, no critique history, no gate summary. The whole point is that these checks are anchored to the issue, not the plan.

### 4. Prompt builders for single-check review

In `megaplan/prompts/review.py`, add:
- `single_check_review_prompt(state, plan_dir, root, check, output_path)` — mirrors `single_check_critique_prompt` — single focused prompt for one check.
- `_write_single_check_review_template(plan_dir, state, check, filename)` — pre-populates the output file with the check's ID.
- `_build_review_checks_template(plan_dir, state, checks)` — builds the per-check template list.

Keep `_review_prompt()` as-is. It still runs as the "criteria_verdict" check in heavy mode.

### 5. Handler integration

In `megaplan/handlers.py` `handle_review()`:
- Read `configured_robustness(state)`
- If `standard` or `light` → run the existing single-call review unchanged
- If `heavy` → run the mechanical pre-checks, then run `parallel_review()` across REVIEW_CHECKS + the existing criteria-based review-as-check. Merge all flags into one `review.json`

The output shape adds a `checks` array alongside the existing `review_verdict`, `criteria`, `issues`, `rework_items`, `task_verdicts`, `sense_check_verdicts`, `summary`. Back-compat: existing consumers that only look at `review_verdict` / `rework_items` keep working.

### 6. Feedback loop — how flags flow back

This is the tricky part. The review runs after execute. If it raises blocking flags, where does control go?

**Decision: reuse the existing gate → revise/re-execute loop, not a new review-specific loop.**

Concretely:
- If `review_verdict == "needs_rework"` with blocking flags, the handler writes them to `rework_items` as today.
- The execute loop in `evals/run_evals.py` already handles `needs_rework` by going back to revise or re-execute based on the rework type. No new routing logic.
- Add a new field `rework_directive.source = "review_{check_id}"` so the executor knows the provenance and can prioritize.
- **Max review iterations cap at 2** (configurable). After 2 rounds of needs_rework from the review, escalate. Otherwise the same check can fire forever on tasks the model can't solve.
- Review flags **cannot re-raise concerns the gate already settled**. Before raising a coverage/placement/parity flag, the review check must check `gate.settled_decisions` — if the concern matches a settled decision, downgrade to "significant" (info) rather than "blocking". This prevents the gate-override deadlock we hit before.

### 7. Severity ladder (mirrors critique)

Each review flag has:
- `blocking` — triggers `needs_rework`, forces another revise/execute pass
- `significant` — logged, shown to gate for future iterations, doesn't auto-block
- `minor` — info only, aggregated into summary

### 8. Flag tracking across review iterations

Mirror critique's flag registry. Each review check's findings get IDs like `REVIEW-COV-001`. Status transitions open → verified → resolved across iterations, just like critique flags. Read `megaplan/_core` flag registry helpers — reuse them, don't duplicate.

### 9. Schema updates

Add a review checks schema fragment alongside the existing review schema in `schemas/` (wherever step schemas live — look at `STEP_SCHEMA_FILENAMES` in `megaplan/workers.py`). The schema should allow the existing top-level fields PLUS a new `checks` array with per-check findings.

### 10. Tests

- Unit tests for `REVIEW_CHECKS` structure and `checks_for_robustness()` behavior
- Unit test that standard robustness still calls the single-call review (regression guard)
- Unit test for mechanical pre-checks (source_touch, diff_size_sanity)
- Integration test: feed a known-bad patch through heavy review, assert it raises the expected flag
- Integration test: feed a known-good patch through heavy review, assert no blocking flags

Use the existing test infrastructure patterns.

## Generalization beyond review

While implementing, keep these generalizations in mind (don't implement them now, but design so they're easy later):

1. **`checks_for_robustness()` pattern** should be extractable to a shared utility — same function body in checks.py and review_checks.py should be one function.
2. **Parallel focused-agent runner** (`parallel_critique.py` + `parallel_review.py`) should be refactorable to a single `parallel_phase_runner.py` that both delegate to. Not in this PR, but leave clean seams.
3. **Template-file-with-preloaded-IDs pattern** should be documented as "the preferred way to get structured output" in a short comment at the top of `parallel_review.py`.
4. **Mechanical pre-checks should be runnable standalone** so they can be reused by critique, finalize, or any other phase that wants cheap advisory checks.

## Out of scope for this plan

- Automatic golden-patch comparison for false-negative detection (we already have `auto_improve/check_false_negatives.py`)
- Running these checks on tasks that already passed
- A/B evaluation of which checks actually earn their spot (that's a separate measurement exercise)
- Any changes to critique, plan, execute, prep, or finalize phases
- Any changes to standard or light robustness mode
- New top-level phases between review and execute

## Success criteria

- Heavy mode review produces a `checks` array with one entry per REVIEW_CHECK
- Standard mode review produces byte-identical output to today for the same input
- Blocking flags flow through the existing rework loop without new routing logic
- Flag iteration cap prevents infinite review→re-execute loops
- Unit + integration tests pass
- Code follows the same patterns as `parallel_critique.py` and `checks.py` — a reader familiar with critique should find nothing surprising in the review code

- MODIFIED: `megaplan/handlers.py` `handle_review()` (branch on robustness)
- MODIFIED: `schemas/review.json` (or equivalent — add checks array)
- NEW: tests under `tests/` matching existing patterns

  "meta_commentary": "Heavy-mode robust review mirrors robust critique. Three critical invariants dominate execution: (1) standard mode must be byte-identical \\u2014 the regression test compares bytes, not keys; any stray whitespace in _review_prompt or the standard handler branch fails it. (2) collect_git_diff_patch MUST include untracked files (FLAG-007 lock-down) \\u2014 use `git add -N .` + `git diff HEAD` + `git reset` because `validate_execution_evidence` at megaplan/evaluation.py:109-175 treats untracked files as real work. (3) heavy_criteria_review_prompt is built from scratch (not a _review_prompt wrapper) and must exclude plan/plan-metadata/gate-summary/execution-summary/execution-audit (FLAG-005/006 lock-down). The grep-assertion test in T11(f) is the load-bearing regression guard \\u2014 do not weaken it. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: i checked the sign-in callback path again. `submissionslayout` already wires `usescoring(..., { onrequiresignin: () => pagemachine.dispatch({ type: 'open_sign_in_modal' }) })` at `src/pages/submissionslayout.tsx:38-40`, and `scorepanel` still has its own `onrequiresignin` prop. the revised step 4.2 is acceptable only if it explicitly rewires that prop to `pagemachine.dispatch`; otherwise removing local modal state would orphan the direct ui callback. the plan hints at this, but it remains easy to misread because the scoring-context callback and the scorepanel prop are separate mechanisms. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)

        Idea:
# Robust Review Phase for Megaplan (Heavy Mode Only)

## Context

Megaplan is a planning/execution harness with phases: prep → plan → critique → gate → (revise → critique → gate)* → finalize → execute → review. Critique already has a "robust" mode where it runs multiple focused checks in parallel (`megaplan/parallel_critique.py` + `megaplan/checks.py`), each as an independent sub-agent with its own session. We validated on ~15 random SWE-bench failures that the current single-call review phase misses ~65% of salvageable failures because it anchors to the plan's success criteria instead of the original issue text, and because it evaluates everything in one call without forced enumeration.

## Goal

Add a **robust review** mode that mirrors the robust critique architecture: multiple focused, parallel, issue-anchored checks that produce structured flags with rework directives, which feed back through the existing gate → revise/re-execute loop.

**Standard robustness mode must remain exactly as today.** This design is heavy-mode only.

## What exists today (don't break)

- `megaplan/prompts/review.py` — single-call review prompt; validates execution against finalize.json criteria/task_verdicts/sense_check_verdicts. Works well as the baseline. Keep it.
- `megaplan/prompts/critique.py` + `megaplan/parallel_critique.py` + `megaplan/checks.py` — the pattern we're mirroring. Read these to understand the architecture.
- `megaplan/handlers.py` `handle_review()` — entry point. Currently calls a single worker.
- The gate → revise/re-execute loop already exists with max iteration cap and ESCALATE semantics.
- `intent_and_notes_block(state)` contains the original issue text — crucial input for issue-anchored checks.

## What needs to exist

### 1. New check table for review

New file `megaplan/review_checks.py` mirroring `megaplan/checks.py`. Define `REVIEW_CHECKS` with these entries (core + extended tiers). Each check has `id`, `question`, `guidance`, `category`, `default_severity`, `tier`:

**Core tier (always run in heavy mode):**
- `coverage` — Enumerate every concrete failing example, symptom, or "X should Y" statement in the issue. For each, point to the specific line in the diff that addresses it. Flag any not covered. Subsumes intent/opt-in/default and accepted-changes concerns.
- `placement` — Trace the call path from the failing input to where we made the change. Is the fix at the source of the bug or a downstream consumer? Would a maintainer put it here or elsewhere?
- `parity` — Find 2–3 structurally similar classes/functions in the same module. Enumerate the methods/overrides/conventions they share. Does our new code follow the same patterns? If not, why not? (Highest new-leverage check.)
- `simplicity` — What's the smallest possible change that would make the reported issue's example work? Compare against our diff. Every extra line must justify its existence against a specific requirement in the issue.

**Extended tier (heavy mode with extended robustness):**
- `dead_guard` — For every new `if x:` / `try:` block added by the patch, trace whether the guarded branch is actually reachable given how callers invoke this function. Flag guards that never fire.
- `adjacent_calls` — What other code calls the changed function or uses the same pattern? Would the same bug affect them?
- `parser_source` — If the bug is in parsing/transformation, is the fix at the source of the bug or at a downstream consumer?
- `return_value_downstream` — For any value the patch returns or constructs in an error path, trace where it flows next. Will the downstream consumer handle it correctly?

Also include a `checks_for_robustness()` function mirroring `megaplan/checks.py`.

### 2. Mechanical pre-checks (no LLM, run before parallel review)

Add a new module `megaplan/review_mechanical.py` or inline in handlers:
- `source_touch` — Does the diff touch at least one non-test file in the package? Reject test-only diffs.
- `diff_size_sanity` — Compare diff size (lines/hunks/files) to a rough expectation from the issue (number of code snippets × ~10 lines). Flag >3x or <0.3x.
- `dead_guard_static` — For new `if x:` blocks, AST-parse the changed file, find the enclosing function, check whether `x` has a callable signature at the call site where it could be None/False. This is a heuristic; don't overreach.

These are **advisory flags**, not blocks. They populate a `pre_check_flags` array that the LLM review checks see as input.

### 3. Parallel review runner

New file `megaplan/parallel_review.py`, mirroring `megaplan/parallel_critique.py`. Same architecture: `ThreadPoolExecutor`, one agent per check, each with its own session via `SessionDB`, writes findings to a pre-populated template slot. Concurrency of 2 (same as critique). Include the MiniMax→OpenRouter fallback pattern.

Important: each check agent gets **the original issue text + the full git diff + the finalize.json artifacts**, nothing else. No plan, no critique history, no gate summary. The whole point is that these checks are anchored to the issue, not the plan.

### 4. Prompt builders for single-check review

In `megaplan/prompts/review.py`, add:
- `single_check_review_prompt(state, plan_dir, root, check, output_path)` — mirrors `single_check_critique_prompt` — single focused prompt for one check.
- `_write_single_check_review_template(plan_dir, state, check, filename)` — pre-populates the output file with the check's ID.
- `_build_review_checks_template(plan_dir, state, checks)` — builds the per-check template list.

Keep `_review_prompt()` as-is. It still runs as the "criteria_verdict" check in heavy mode.

### 5. Handler integration

In `megaplan/handlers.py` `handle_review()`:
- Read `configured_robustness(state)`
- If `standard` or `light` → run the existing single-call review unchanged
- If `heavy` → run the mechanical pre-checks, then run `parallel_review()` across REVIEW_CHECKS + the existing criteria-based review-as-check. Merge all flags into one `review.json`

The output shape adds a `checks` array alongside the existing `review_verdict`, `criteria`, `issues`, `rework_items`, `task_verdicts`, `sense_check_verdicts`, `summary`. Back-compat: existing consumers that only look at `review_verdict` / `rework_items` keep working.

### 6. Feedback loop — how flags flow back

This is the tricky part. The review runs after execute. If it raises blocking flags, where does control go?

**Decision: reuse the existing gate → revise/re-execute loop, not a new review-specific loop.**

Concretely:
- If `review_verdict == "needs_rework"` with blocking flags, the handler writes them to `rework_items` as today.
- The execute loop in `evals/run_evals.py` already handles `needs_rework` by going back to revise or re-execute based on the rework type. No new routing logic.
- Add a new field `rework_directive.source = "review_{check_id}"` so the executor knows the provenance and can prioritize.
- **Max review iterations cap at 2** (configurable). After 2 rounds of needs_rework from the review, escalate. Otherwise the same check can fire forever on tasks the model can't solve.
- Review flags **cannot re-raise concerns the gate already settled**. Before raising a coverage/placement/parity flag, the review check must check `gate.settled_decisions` — if the concern matches a settled decision, downgrade to "significant" (info) rather than "blocking". This prevents the gate-override deadlock we hit before.

### 7. Severity ladder (mirrors critique)

Each review flag has:
- `blocking` — triggers `needs_rework`, forces another revise/execute pass
- `significant` — logged, shown to gate for future iterations, doesn't auto-block
- `minor` — info only, aggregated into summary

### 8. Flag tracking across review iterations

Mirror critique's flag registry. Each review check's findings get IDs like `REVIEW-COV-001`. Status transitions open → verified → resolved across iterations, just like critique flags. Read `megaplan/_core` flag registry helpers — reuse them, don't duplicate.

### 9. Schema updates

Add a review checks schema fragment alongside the existing review schema in `schemas/` (wherever step schemas live — look at `STEP_SCHEMA_FILENAMES` in `megaplan/workers.py`). The schema should allow the existing top-level fields PLUS a new `checks` array with per-check findings.

### 10. Tests

- Unit tests for `REVIEW_CHECKS` structure and `checks_for_robustness()` behavior
- Unit test that standard robustness still calls the single-call review (regression guard)
- Unit test for mechanical pre-checks (source_touch, diff_size_sanity)
- Integration test: feed a known-bad patch through heavy review, assert it raises the expected flag
- Integration test: feed a known-good patch through heavy review, assert no blocking flags

Use the existing test infrastructure patterns.

## Generalization beyond review

While implementing, keep these generalizations in mind (don't implement them now, but design so they're easy later):

1. **`checks_for_robustness()` pattern** should be extractable to a shared utility — same function body in checks.py and review_checks.py should be one function.
2. **Parallel focused-agent runner** (`parallel_critique.py` + `parallel_review.py`) should be refactorable to a single `parallel_phase_runner.py` that both delegate to. Not in this PR, but leave clean seams.
3. **Template-file-with-preloaded-IDs pattern** should be documented as "the preferred way to get structured output" in a short comment at the top of `parallel_review.py`.
4. **Mechanical pre-checks should be runnable standalone** so they can be reused by critique, finalize, or any other phase that wants cheap advisory checks.

## Out of scope for this plan

- Automatic golden-patch comparison for false-negative detection (we already have `auto_improve/check_false_negatives.py`)
- Running these checks on tasks that already passed
- A/B evaluation of which checks actually earn their spot (that's a separate measurement exercise)
- Any changes to critique, plan, execute, prep, or finalize phases
- Any changes to standard or light robustness mode
- New top-level phases between review and execute

## Success criteria

- Heavy mode review produces a `checks` array with one entry per REVIEW_CHECK
- Standard mode review produces byte-identical output to today for the same input
- Blocking flags flow through the existing rework loop without new routing logic
- Flag iteration cap prevents infinite review→re-execute loops
- Unit + integration tests pass
- Code follows the same patterns as `parallel_critique.py` and `checks.py` — a reader familiar with critique should find nothing surprising in the review code

- NEW: `megaplan/parallel_review.py`
- NEW: `megaplan/review_mechanical.py` (or inline in handlers)
- MODIFIED: `megaplan/prompts/review.py` (add single-check helpers, don't touch existing `_review_prompt`)
- MODIFIED: `megaplan/handlers.py` `handle_review()` (branch on robustness)
- MODIFIED: `schemas/review.json` (or equivalent — add checks array)
- NEW: tests under `tests/` matching existing patterns

  "meta_commentary": "Heavy-mode robust review mirrors robust critique. Three critical invariants dominate execution: (1) standard mode must be byte-identical \\u2014 the regression test compares bytes, not keys; any stray whitespace in _review_prompt or the standard handler branch fails it. (2) collect_git_diff_patch MUST include untracked files (FLAG-007 lock-down) \\u2014 use `git add -N .` + `git diff HEAD` + `git reset` because `validate_execution_evidence` at megaplan/evaluation.py:109-175 treats untracked files as real work. (3) heavy_criteria_review_prompt is built from scratch (not a _review_prompt wrapper) and must exclude plan/plan-metadata/gate-summary/execution-summary/execution-audit (FLAG-005/006 lock-down). The grep-assertion test in T11(f) is the load-bearing regression guard \\u2014 do not weaken it. The plan deliberately diverges from the brief's literal 'keep _review_prompt as criteria_verdict' wording (GATE-SD-009) because the brief is internally contradictory: '_review_prompt as-is' and 'issue-anchored context only' cannot both hold. Document this in the new prompt's docstring. Execution order can parallelize: T2/T3/T4 are independent after T1; T5/T6 parallel after T1/T3; T7 depends on T3+T5; T8 on T4+T5+T7; T9 on T5; T10 on T2+T6+T8+T9; T11/T12 last. Thread the new `robustness` parameter through BOTH handle_review callsites \\u2014 easy to miss one. parallel_review must use _toolsets_for_phase('review') (file-only), NOT critique's file+web \\u2014 review is read-only. REVIEW_CHECKS stays at exactly 8 entries; criteria-verdict is scheduled as an extra future inside parallel_review via a `_run_criteria_verdict` helper, not as a registry entry. Flag ids use REVIEW-<CHECK_ID>-NNN. Share _synthesize_flags_from_checks between critique and review \\u2014 don't copy-paste. The sentinel task_id='REVIEW' in heavy-mode rework items must be tolerated by _merge_review_verdicts (which only strict-checks task_verdicts). No changes to evals/run_evals.py routing \\u2014 the existing gate\\u2192revise/execute loop handles needs_rework unchanged.",

by_id: dict[str, FlagRecord] = {flag[\"id\"]: flag for flag in registry[\"flags\"]}\n+    for res in resolutions:\n+        flag_id = res.get(\"flag_id\", \"\")\n+        action = res.get(\"action\",         Idea:
# Robust Review Phase for Megaplan (Heavy Mode Only)

## Context

Megaplan is a planning/execution harness with phases: prep → plan → critique → gate → (revise → critique → gate)* → finalize → execute → review. Critique already has a "robust" mode where it runs multiple focused checks in parallel (`megaplan/parallel_critique.py` + `megaplan/checks.py`), each as an independent sub-agent with its own session. We validated on ~15 random SWE-bench failures that the current single-call review phase misses ~65% of salvageable failures because it anchors to the plan's success criteria instead of the original issue text, and because it evaluates everything in one call without forced enumeration.

## Goal

Add a **robust review** mode that mirrors the robust critique architecture: multiple focused, parallel, issue-anchored checks that produce structured flags with rework directives, which feed back through the existing gate → revise/re-execute loop.

**Standard robustness mode must remain exactly as today.** This design is heavy-mode only.

## What exists today (don't break)

- `megaplan/prompts/review.py` — single-call review prompt; validates execution against finalize.json criteria/task_verdicts/sense_check_verdicts. Works well as the baseline. Keep it.
- `megaplan/prompts/critique.py` + `megaplan/parallel_critique.py` + `megaplan/checks.py` — the pattern we're mirroring. Read these to understand the architecture.
- `megaplan/handlers.py` `handle_review()` — entry point. Currently calls a single worker.
- The gate → revise/re-execute loop already exists with max iteration cap and ESCALATE semantics.
- `intent_and_notes_block(state)` contains the original issue text — crucial input for issue-anchored checks.

## What needs to exist

### 1. New check table for review

New file `megaplan/review_checks.py` mirroring `megaplan/checks.py`. Define `REVIEW_CHECKS` with these entries (core + extended tiers). Each check has `id`, `question`, `guidance`, `category`, `default_severity`, `tier`:

**Core tier (always run in heavy mode):**
- `coverage` — Enumerate every concrete failing example, symptom, or "X should Y" statement in the issue. For each, point to the specific line in the diff that addresses it. Flag any not covered. Subsumes intent/opt-in/default and accepted-changes concerns.
- `placement` — Trace the call path from the failing input to where we made the change. Is the fix at the source of the bug or a downstream consumer? Would a maintainer put it here or elsewhere?
- `parity` — Find 2–3 structurally similar classes/functions in the same module. Enumerate the methods/overrides/conventions they share. Does our new code follow the same patterns? If not, why not? (Highest new-leverage check.)
- `simplicity` — What's the smallest possible change that would make the reported issue's example work? Compare against our diff. Every extra line must justify its existence against a specific requirement in the issue.

**Extended tier (heavy mode with extended robustness):**
- `dead_guard` — For every new `if x:` / `try:` block added by the patch, trace whether the guarded branch is actually reachable given how callers invoke this function. Flag guards that never fire.
- `adjacent_calls` — What other code calls the changed function or uses the same pattern? Would the same bug affect them?
- `parser_source` — If the bug is in parsing/transformation, is the fix at the source of the bug or at a downstream consumer?
- `return_value_downstream` — For any value the patch returns or constructs in an error path, trace where it flows next. Will the downstream consumer handle it correctly?

Also include a `checks_for_robustness()` function mirroring `megaplan/checks.py`.

### 2. Mechanical pre-checks (no LLM, run before parallel review)

Add a new module `megaplan/review_mechanical.py` or inline in handlers:
- `source_touch` — Does the diff touch at least one non-test file in the package? Reject test-only diffs.
- `diff_size_sanity` — Compare diff size (lines/hunks/files) to a rough expectation from the issue (number of code snippets × ~10 lines). Flag >3x or <0.3x.
- `dead_guard_static` — For new `if x:` blocks, AST-parse the changed file, find the enclosing function, check whether `x` has a callable signature at the call site where it could be None/False. This is a heuristic; don't overreach.

These are **advisory flags**, not blocks. They populate a `pre_check_flags` array that the LLM review checks see as input.

### 3. Parallel review runner

New file `megaplan/parallel_review.py`, mirroring `megaplan/parallel_critique.py`. Same architecture: `ThreadPoolExecutor`, one agent per check, each with its own session via `SessionDB`, writes findings to a pre-populated template slot. Concurrency of 2 (same as critique). Include the MiniMax→OpenRouter fallback pattern.

Important: each check agent gets **the original issue text + the full git diff + the finalize.json artifacts**, nothing else. No plan, no critique history, no gate summary. The whole point is that these checks are anchored to the issue, not the plan.

### 4. Prompt builders for single-check review

In `megaplan/prompts/review.py`, add:
- `single_check_review_prompt(state, plan_dir, root, check, output_path)` — mirrors `single_check_critique_prompt` — single focused prompt for one check.
- `_write_single_check_review_template(plan_dir, state, check, filename)` — pre-populates the output file with the check's ID.
- `_build_review_checks_template(plan_dir, state, checks)` — builds the per-check template list.

Keep `_review_prompt()` as-is. It still runs as the "criteria_verdict" check in heavy mode.

### 5. Handler integration

In `megaplan/handlers.py` `handle_review()`:
- Read `configured_robustness(state)`
- If `standard` or `light` → run the existing single-call review unchanged
- If `heavy` → run the mechanical pre-checks, then run `parallel_review()` across REVIEW_CHECKS + the existing criteria-based review-as-check. Merge all flags into one `review.json`

The output shape adds a `checks` array alongside the existing `review_verdict`, `criteria`, `issues`, `rework_items`, `task_verdicts`, `sense_check_verdicts`, `summary`. Back-compat: existing consumers that only look at `review_verdict` / `rework_items` keep working.

### 6. Feedback loop — how flags flow back

This is the tricky part. The review runs after execute. If it raises blocking flags, where does control go?

**Decision: reuse the existing gate → revise/re-execute loop, not a new review-specific loop.**

Concretely:
- If `review_verdict == "needs_rework"` with blocking flags, the handler writes them to `rework_items` as today.
- The execute loop in `evals/run_evals.py` already handles `needs_rework` by going back to revise or re-execute based on the rework type. No new routing logic.
- Add a new field `rework_directive.source = "review_{check_id}"` so the executor knows the provenance and can prioritize.
- **Max review iterations cap at 2** (configurable). After 2 rounds of needs_rework from the review, escalate. Otherwise the same check can fire forever on tasks the model can't solve.
- Review flags **cannot re-raise concerns the gate already settled**. Before raising a coverage/placement/parity flag, the review check must check `gate.settled_decisions` — if the concern matches a settled decision, downgrade to "significant" (info) rather than "blocking". This prevents the gate-override deadlock we hit before.

### 7. Severity ladder (mirrors critique)

Each review flag has:
- `blocking` — triggers `needs_rework`, forces another revise/execute pass
- `significant` — logged, shown to gate for future iterations, doesn't auto-block
- `minor` — info only, aggregated into summary

### 8. Flag tracking across review iterations

Mirror critique's flag registry. Each review check's findings get IDs like `REVIEW-COV-001`. Status transitions open → verified → resolved across iterations, just like critique flags. Read `megaplan/_core` flag registry helpers — reuse them, don't duplicate.

### 9. Schema updates

Add a review checks schema fragment alongside the existing review schema in `schemas/` (wherever step schemas live — look at `STEP_SCHEMA_FILENAMES` in `megaplan/workers.py`). The schema should allow the existing top-level fields PLUS a new `checks` array with per-check findings.

### 10. Tests

- Unit tests for `REVIEW_CHECKS` structure and `checks_for_robustness()` behavior
- Unit test that standard robustness still calls the single-call review (regression guard)
- Unit test for mechanical pre-checks (source_touch, diff_size_sanity)
- Integration test: feed a known-bad patch through heavy review, assert it raises the expected flag
- Integration test: feed a known-good patch through heavy review, assert no blocking flags

Use the existing test infrastructure patterns.

## Generalization beyond review

While implementing, keep these generalizations in mind (don't implement them now, but design so they're easy later):

1. **`checks_for_robustness()` pattern** should be extractable to a shared utility — same function body in checks.py and review_checks.py should be one function.
2. **Parallel focused-agent runner** (`parallel_critique.py` + `parallel_review.py`) should be refactorable to a single `parallel_phase_runner.py` that both delegate to. Not in this PR, but leave clean seams.
3. **Template-file-with-preloaded-IDs pattern** should be documented as "the preferred way to get structured output" in a short comment at the top of `parallel_review.py`.
4. **Mechanical pre-checks should be runnable standalone** so they can be reused by critique, finalize, or any other phase that wants cheap advisory checks.

## Out of scope for this plan

- Automatic golden-patch comparison for false-negative detection (we already have `auto_improve/check_false_negatives.py`)
- Running these checks on tasks that already passed
- A/B evaluation of which checks actually earn their spot (that's a separate measurement exercise)
- Any changes to critique, plan, execute, prep, or finalize phases
- Any changes to standard or light robustness mode
- New top-level phases between review and execute

## Success criteria

- Heavy mode review produces a `checks` array with one entry per REVIEW_CHECK
- Standard mode review produces byte-identical output to today for the same input
- Blocking flags flow through the existing rework loop without new routing logic
- Flag iteration cap prevents infinite review→re-execute loops
- Unit + integration tests pass
- Code follows the same patterns as `parallel_critique.py` and `checks.py` — a reader familiar with critique should find nothing surprising in the review code

- MODIFIED: `megaplan/handlers.py` `handle_review()` (branch on robustness)
- MODIFIED: `schemas/review.json` (or equivalent — add checks array)
- NEW: tests under `tests/` matching existing patterns

(2) collect_git_diff_patch MUST include untracked files (FLAG-007 lock-down) \\u2014 use `git add -N .` + `git diff HEAD` + `git reset` because `validate_execution_evidence` at megaplan/evaluation.py:109-175 treats untracked files as real work. The grep-assertion test in T11(f) is the load-bearing regression guard \\u2014 do not weaken it. The plan deliberately diverges from the brief's literal 'keep _review_prompt as criteria_verdict' wording (GATE-SD-009) because the brief is internally contradictory: '_review_prompt as-is' and 'issue-anchored context only' cannot both hold. Document this in the new prompt's docstring. Execution order can parallelize: T2/T3/T4 are independent after T1; T5/T6 parallel after T1/T3; T7 depends on T3+T5; T8 on T4+T5+T7; T9 on T5; T10 on T2+T6+T8+T9; T11/T12 last. Thread the new `robustness` parameter through BOTH handle_review callsites \\u2014 easy to miss one. parallel_review must use _toolsets_for_phase('review') (file-only), NOT critique's file+web \\u2014 review is read-only. REVIEW_CHECKS stays at exactly 8 entries; criteria-verdict is scheduled as an extra future inside parallel_review via a `_run_criteria_verdict` helper, not as a registry entry. Flag ids use REVIEW-<CHECK_ID>-NNN. Share _synthesize_flags_from_checks between critique and review \\u2014 don't copy-paste. The sentinel task_id='REVIEW' in heavy-mode rework items must be tolerated by _merge_review_verdicts (which only strict-checks task_verdicts).         Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the mobile action-surface plan is technically incorrect for the current component structure. `submissioncarouselcard` hides `footercontent` below the `md` breakpoint (`src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx:219`), while `submissionscarouselpage` also hides the score panel on mobile via `asidecontent={<div classname="hidden md:block">...` and passes `hideactions` to `scorepanel` at `src/pages/submissionscarouselpage.tsx:250-263,347`. reusing that pattern for entrypage would leave mobile users with no navigation controls and no scoring controls. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: i checked the sign-in callback path again. `submissionslayout` already wires `usescoring(..., { onrequiresignin: () => pagemachine.dispatch({ type: 'open_sign_in_modal' }) })` at `src/pages/submissionslayout.tsx:38-40`, and `scorepanel` still has its own `onrequiresignin` prop. the revised step 4.2 is acceptable only if it explicitly rewires that prop to `pagemachine.dispatch`; otherwise removing local modal state would orphan the direct ui callback. the plan hints at this, but it remains easy to misread because the scoring-context callback and the scorepanel prop are separate mechanisms. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the new diff-helper proposal against the repo's existing execution-evidence semantics. `validate_execution_evidence()` in `megaplan/evaluation.py:109-175` uses `git status --short`, so untracked files count as real changed files today, but step 3 proposes `collect_git_diff_patch(project_dir)` via `git diff head`; by git behavior that will not include untracked files, so heavy review could miss exactly the newly created files that execute and review already treat as part of the patch. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-success-criteria-well-prioritized-and-verifiable: are the success criteria well-prioritized and verifiable?: mobile behavior is still under-prioritized relative to the actual risk in this repo. the user constraint specifically mentions the mobile bottom nav, but the only mobile-specific success criterion left is 'mobile scoring ux is coherent' at `info` level. given that the plan currently removes the working mobile nav, there should be at least a `should` or `must` criterion stating that mobile users retain navigation and scoring controls. _run_attempt(agent, output_path)\n+                except Exception as fallback_exc:\n+                    raise CliError(\n+                        \"worker_error\",\n+                        (\n+                            \"Parallel review criteria verdict failed \"\n+                            f\"(both MiniMax and OpenRouter): primary={_failure_reason(exc)}; \"\n+                            f\"fallback={_failure_reason(fallback_exc)}\"\n+                        ),\n+                    ) from fallback_exc\n+            else:\n+                raise\n+        else:\n+            raise\n+    return payload, cost_usd, pt, ct, tt\n+\n+\n+def run_parallel_review(\n+    state: PlanState,\n+    plan_dir: Path,\n+    *,\n+    root: Path,\n+    model: str | None,\n+    checks: tuple[Any, ...],\n+    pre_check_flags: list[dict[str, Any]],\n+    max_concurrent: int | None = None,\n+) -> WorkerResult:\n+    started = time.monotonic()\n+    schema = read_json(schemas_root(root) / STEP_SCHEMA_FILENAMES[\"review\"])\n+    project_dir = Path(state[\"config\"][\"project_dir\"])\n+    results: list[tuple[dict[str, Any], list[str], list[str]] | None] = [None] * len(checks)\n+    criteria_payload: dict[str, Any] | None = None\n+    total_cost = 0.0\n+    total_prompt_tokens = 0\n+    total_completion_tokens = 0\n+    total_tokens = 0\n+\n+    real_stdout = sys.stdout\n+    sys.stdout = sys.stderr\n+    try:\n+        total_futures = len(checks) + 1\n+        concurrency = min(\n+            max_concurrent or get_effective(\"orchestration\", \"max_critique_concurrency\"),\n+            total_futures,\n+        )\n+        with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=concurrency) as executor:\n+            future_map = {\n+                executor.submit(\n+                    _run_check,\n+                    index,\n+                    check,\n+                    state=state,\n+                    plan_dir=plan_dir,\n+                    root=root,\n+                    model=model,\n+                    schema=schema,\n+                    project_dir=project_dir,\n+                    pre_check_flags=pre_check_flags,\n+                ): (\"check\", index)\n+                for index, check in enumerate(checks)\n+            }\n+            future_map[\n+                executor.submit(\n+                    _run_criteria_verdict,\n+                    state=state,\n+                    plan_dir=plan_dir,\n+                    root=root,\n+                    model=model,\n+                    schema=schema,\n+                    project_dir=project_dir,\n+                )\n+            ] = (\"criteria\", None)\n+            for future in as_completed(future_map):\n+                kind, _index = future_map[future]\n+                result = future.result()\n+                if kind == \"criteria\":\n+                    criteria_payload, cost_usd, pt, ct, tt = result\n+                else:\n+                    index, check_payload, verified_ids, disputed_ids, cost_usd, pt, ct, tt = result\n+                    results[index] = (check_payload, verified_ids, disputed_ids)\n+                total_cost += cost_usd\n+                total_prompt_tokens += pt\n+                total_completion_tokens += ct\n+                total_tokens += tt\n+    finally:\n+        sys.stdout = real_stdout\n+\n+    ordered_checks: list[dict[str, Any]] = []\n+    verified_groups: list[list[str]] = []\n+    disputed_groups: list[list[str]] = []\n+    for item in results:\n+        if item is None:\n+            raise CliError(\"worker_error\", \"Parallel review did not return all check results\")\n+        check_payload, verified_ids, disputed_ids = item\n+        ordered_checks.append(check_payload)\n+        verified_groups.append(verified_ids)\n+        disputed_groups.append(disputed_ids)\n+\n+    if criteria_payload is None:\n+        raise CliError(\"worker_error\", \"Parallel review did not return a criteria verdict payload\")\n+\n+    disputed_flag_ids = _merge_unique(disputed_groups)\n+    disputed_set = set(disputed_flag_ids)\n+    verified_flag_ids = [flag_id for flag_id in _merge_unique(verified_groups) if flag_id not in disputed_set]\n+    return WorkerResult(\n+        payload={\n+            \"checks\": ordered_checks,\n+            \"verified_flag_ids\": verified_flag_ids,\n+            \"disputed_flag_ids\": disputed_flag_ids,\n+            \"criteria_payload\": criteria_payload,\n+        },\n+        raw_output=\"parallel\",\n+        duration_ms=int((time.monotonic() - started) * 1000),\n+        cost_usd=total_cost,\n+        session_id=None,\n+        prompt_tokens=total_prompt_tokens,\n+        completion_tokens=total_completion_tokens,\n+        total_tokens=total_tokens,\n+    )\n*** End Patch\n"
}

        Idea:
# Robust Review Phase for Megaplan (Heavy Mode Only)

## Context

Megaplan is a planning/execution harness with phases: prep → plan → critique → gate → (revise → critique → gate)* → finalize → execute → review. Critique already has a "robust" mode where it runs multiple focused checks in parallel (`megaplan/parallel_critique.py` + `megaplan/checks.py`), each as an independent sub-agent with its own session. We validated on ~15 random SWE-bench failures that the current single-call review phase misses ~65% of salvageable failures because it anchors to the plan's success criteria instead of the original issue text, and because it evaluates everything in one call without forced enumeration.

## Goal

Add a **robust review** mode that mirrors the robust critique architecture: multiple focused, parallel, issue-anchored checks that produce structured flags with rework directives, which feed back through the existing gate → revise/re-execute loop.

**Standard robustness mode must remain exactly as today.** This design is heavy-mode only.

## What exists today (don't break)

- `megaplan/prompts/review.py` — single-call review prompt; validates execution against finalize.json criteria/task_verdicts/sense_check_verdicts. Works well as the baseline. Keep it.
- `megaplan/prompts/critique.py` + `megaplan/parallel_critique.py` + `megaplan/checks.py` — the pattern we're mirroring. Read these to understand the architecture.
- `megaplan/handlers.py` `handle_review()` — entry point. Currently calls a single worker.
- The gate → revise/re-execute loop already exists with max iteration cap and ESCALATE semantics.
- `intent_and_notes_block(state)` contains the original issue text — crucial input for issue-anchored checks.

## What needs to exist

### 1. New check table for review

New file `megaplan/review_checks.py` mirroring `megaplan/checks.py`. Define `REVIEW_CHECKS` with these entries (core + extended tiers). Each check has `id`, `question`, `guidance`, `category`, `default_severity`, `tier`:

**Core tier (always run in heavy mode):**
- `coverage` — Enumerate every concrete failing example, symptom, or "X should Y" statement in the issue. For each, point to the specific line in the diff that addresses it. Flag any not covered. Subsumes intent/opt-in/default and accepted-changes concerns.
- `placement` — Trace the call path from the failing input to where we made the change. Is the fix at the source of the bug or a downstream consumer? Would a maintainer put it here or elsewhere?
- `parity` — Find 2–3 structurally similar classes/functions in the same module. Enumerate the methods/overrides/conventions they share. Does our new code follow the same patterns? If not, why not? (Highest new-leverage check.)
- `simplicity` — What's the smallest possible change that would make the reported issue's example work? Compare against our diff. Every extra line must justify its existence against a specific requirement in the issue.

**Extended tier (heavy mode with extended robustness):**
- `dead_guard` — For every new `if x:` / `try:` block added by the patch, trace whether the guarded branch is actually reachable given how callers invoke this function. Flag guards that never fire.
- `adjacent_calls` — What other code calls the changed function or uses the same pattern? Would the same bug affect them?
- `parser_source` — If the bug is in parsing/transformation, is the fix at the source of the bug or at a downstream consumer?
- `return_value_downstream` — For any value the patch returns or constructs in an error path, trace where it flows next. Will the downstream consumer handle it correctly?

Also include a `checks_for_robustness()` function mirroring `megaplan/checks.py`.

### 2. Mechanical pre-checks (no LLM, run before parallel review)

Add a new module `megaplan/review_mechanical.py` or inline in handlers:
- `source_touch` — Does the diff touch at least one non-test file in the package? Reject test-only diffs.
- `diff_size_sanity` — Compare diff size (lines/hunks/files) to a rough expectation from the issue (number of code snippets × ~10 lines). Flag >3x or <0.3x.
- `dead_guard_static` — For new `if x:` blocks, AST-parse the changed file, find the enclosing function, check whether `x` has a callable signature at the call site where it could be None/False. This is a heuristic; don't overreach.

These are **advisory flags**, not blocks. They populate a `pre_check_flags` array that the LLM review checks see as input.

### 3. Parallel review runner

New file `megaplan/parallel_review.py`, mirroring `megaplan/parallel_critique.py`. Same architecture: `ThreadPoolExecutor`, one agent per check, each with its own session via `SessionDB`, writes findings to a pre-populated template slot. Concurrency of 2 (same as critique). Include the MiniMax→OpenRouter fallback pattern.

Important: each check agent gets **the original issue text + the full git diff + the finalize.json artifacts**, nothing else. No plan, no critique history, no gate summary. The whole point is that these checks are anchored to the issue, not the plan.

### 4. Prompt builders for single-check review

In `megaplan/prompts/review.py`, add:
- `single_check_review_prompt(state, plan_dir, root, check, output_path)` — mirrors `single_check_critique_prompt` — single focused prompt for one check.
- `_write_single_check_review_template(plan_dir, state, check, filename)` — pre-populates the output file with the check's ID.
- `_build_review_checks_template(plan_dir, state, checks)` — builds the per-check template list.

Keep `_review_prompt()` as-is. It still runs as the "criteria_verdict" check in heavy mode.

### 5. Handler integration

In `megaplan/handlers.py` `handle_review()`:
- Read `configured_robustness(state)`
- If `standard` or `light` → run the existing single-call review unchanged
- If `heavy` → run the mechanical pre-checks, then run `parallel_review()` across REVIEW_CHECKS + the existing criteria-based review-as-check. Merge all flags into one `review.json`

The output shape adds a `checks` array alongside the existing `review_verdict`, `criteria`, `issues`, `rework_items`, `task_verdicts`, `sense_check_verdicts`, `summary`. Back-compat: existing consumers that only look at `review_verdict` / `rework_items` keep working.

### 6. Feedback loop — how flags flow back

This is the tricky part. The review runs after execute. If it raises blocking flags, where does control go?

**Decision: reuse the existing gate → revise/re-execute loop, not a new review-specific loop.**

Concretely:
- If `review_verdict == "needs_rework"` with blocking flags, the handler writes them to `rework_items` as today.
- The execute loop in `evals/run_evals.py` already handles `needs_rework` by going back to revise or re-execute based on the rework type. No new routing logic.
- Add a new field `rework_directive.source = "review_{check_id}"` so the executor knows the provenance and can prioritize.
- **Max review iterations cap at 2** (configurable). After 2 rounds of needs_rework from the review, escalate. Otherwise the same check can fire forever on tasks the model can't solve.
- Review flags **cannot re-raise concerns the gate already settled**. Before raising a coverage/placement/parity flag, the review check must check `gate.settled_decisions` — if the concern matches a settled decision, downgrade to "significant" (info) rather than "blocking". This prevents the gate-override deadlock we hit before.

### 7. Severity ladder (mirrors critique)

Each review flag has:
- `blocking` — triggers `needs_rework`, forces another revise/execute pass
- `significant` — logged, shown to gate for future iterations, doesn't auto-block
- `minor` — info only, aggregated into summary

### 8. Flag tracking across review iterations

Mirror critique's flag registry. Each review check's findings get IDs like `REVIEW-COV-001`. Status transitions open → verified → resolved across iterations, just like critique flags. Read `megaplan/_core` flag registry helpers — reuse them, don't duplicate.

### 9. Schema updates

Add a review checks schema fragment alongside the existing review schema in `schemas/` (wherever step schemas live — look at `STEP_SCHEMA_FILENAMES` in `megaplan/workers.py`). The schema should allow the existing top-level fields PLUS a new `checks` array with per-check findings.

### 10. Tests

- Unit tests for `REVIEW_CHECKS` structure and `checks_for_robustness()` behavior
- Unit test that standard robustness still calls the single-call review (regression guard)
- Unit test for mechanical pre-checks (source_touch, diff_size_sanity)
- Integration test: feed a known-bad patch through heavy review, assert it raises the expected flag
- Integration test: feed a known-good patch through heavy review, assert no blocking flags

Use the existing test infrastructure patterns.

## Generalization beyond review

While implementing, keep these generalizations in mind (don't implement them now, but design so they're easy later):

1. **`checks_for_robustness()` pattern** should be extractable to a shared utility — same function body in checks.py and review_checks.py should be one function.
2. **Parallel focused-agent runner** (`parallel_critique.py` + `parallel_review.py`) should be refactorable to a single `parallel_phase_runner.py` that both delegate to. Not in this PR, but leave clean seams.
3. **Template-file-with-preloaded-IDs pattern** should be documented as "the preferred way to get structured output" in a short comment at the top of `parallel_review.py`.
4. **Mechanical pre-checks should be runnable standalone** so they can be reused by critique, finalize, or any other phase that wants cheap advisory checks.

## Out of scope for this plan

- Automatic golden-patch comparison for false-negative detection (we already have `auto_improve/check_false_negatives.py`)
- Running these checks on tasks that already passed
- A/B evaluation of which checks actually earn their spot (that's a separate measurement exercise)
- Any changes to critique, plan, execute, prep, or finalize phases
- Any changes to standard or light robustness mode
- New top-level phases between review and execute

## Success criteria

- Heavy mode review produces a `checks` array with one entry per REVIEW_CHECK
- Standard mode review produces byte-identical output to today for the same input
- Blocking flags flow through the existing rework loop without new routing logic
- Flag iteration cap prevents infinite review→re-execute loops
- Unit + integration tests pass
- Code follows the same patterns as `parallel_critique.py` and `checks.py` — a reader familiar with critique should find nothing surprising in the review code

## Files likely to change

- NEW: `megaplan/review_checks.py`
- NEW: `megaplan/parallel_review.py`
- NEW: `megaplan/review_mechanical.py` (or inline in handlers)
- MODIFIED: `megaplan/prompts/review.py` (add single-check helpers, don't touch existing `_review_prompt`)
- MODIFIED: `megaplan/handlers.py` `handle_review()` (branch on robustness)
- MODIFIED: `schemas/review.json` (or equivalent — add checks array)
- NEW: tests under `tests/` matching existing patterns

      "description": "Create megaplan/review_mechanical.py exposing `run_pre_checks(plan_dir, state, project_dir) -> list[dict]`. Implement three advisory checks: (1) `source_touch` \u2014 diff touches at least one non-test file in the package (reject test-only diffs); (2) `diff_size_sanity` \u2014 compare diff line/hunk/file counts to rough issue expectation, flag >3x or <0.3x (use `collect_git_diff_patch` from T3 for accurate counts); (3) `dead_guard_static` \u2014 AST-parse changed files, for new `if x:` blocks find enclosing function and heuristically check whether x could be None/False at call site. Graceful AST-failure handling. All three are advisory (non-blocking) and return dicts appended to a pre_check_flags array. Module must be importable standalone.",

The implementation uses `collect_git_diff_patch(...)` for diff parsing, emits only advisory `pre_check_flags`-style dicts (`severity` stays in the non-blocking `minor`/`significant` range), and handles AST parse failures gracefully by returning a `dead_guard_static` advisory note instead of raising. Verified by manual temp-repo exercises: one repo with a newly added `if flag:` branch and an unparseable `broken.py` returned `dead_guard_static` findings for the unreachable guard and the parse-failure skip path; a second repo with a test-only diff returned `source_touch` and `diff_size_sanity` advisories as expected.
